# Fixture audit

This is the derive-lane (#2) audit of the fixture corpus. It records the framing of the nine ASCII files of `app/data/ASCII/` and of the ten in-stream USRSEC records, and the results the audited rules give when applied to those records in the four scenarios below. No recorded COBOL output exists, so every expected value here was derived by applying the rules of `copybooks.md`, `arithmetic.md` and the batch programs to the cited records, before the codec, load and batch tests that assert them are written. Code and tests that consume this file are accepted only once the reviewer marks it `Accepted` in the status table of `README.md` (a8, a9; AAP 0.8.6). Every source was read in place at commit `59cc6c2`; no fixture was copied or changed (R16).

## Sources

- Fixtures: `app/data/ASCII/{acctdata,carddata,cardxref,custdata,dailytran,discgrp,tcatbal,trancatg,trantype}.txt` (626 records) and the USRSEC in-stream records `app/jcl/DUSRSECJ.jcl:35-44`.
- Programs: `app/cbl/CBTRN02C.cbl` (`posttran`), `app/cbl/CBACT04C.cbl` (`intcalc`), `app/cbl/CBSTM03A.CBL` and `app/cbl/CBSTM03B.CBL` (`creastmt`), `app/cbl/CBTRN03C.cbl` (`tranrept`), with `app/cpy/COSTM01.CPY`, `app/cpy/CUSTREC.cpy` and `app/cpy/CVTRA07Y.cpy`.
- Job parameters and load order, read only and outside the source inventory (E22): `app/jcl/POSTTRAN.jcl:23-38`, `app/jcl/INTCALC.jcl:22,37-41`, `app/jcl/CREASTMT.JCL:53-54,87-96`, `app/jcl/TRANREPT.jcl:41-48`, `app/proc/TRANREPT.prc:39-46`, the nine load jobs `app/jcl/{ACCTFILE,CARDFILE,XREFFILE,CUSTFILE,DISCGRP,TCATBALF,TRANCATG,TRANTYPE,DUSRSECJ}.jcl` and `scripts/remote_refresh.sh:16-44`.
- Rules: field layouts and codec rules by their IDs in `copybooks.md`, arithmetic by its `ARITH-` IDs in `arithmetic.md`, anomalies by their `E` and `H` IDs in `ESCALATIONS.md` and `HALTS.md`. Program rules of `batch/*.md` are cited by ID where the plan fixes the ID (`CBTRN02C-001` to `CBTRN02C-008`, AAP 0.8.2) and otherwise by COBOL paragraph and source lines, which identify the batch-audit row they correspond to.

## Measurements

Every count below was re-measured at `59cc6c2` from the repository root, with `F` standing for each fixture file:

- Lines: `wc -l app/data/ASCII/*.txt` (626 in total).
- Body length per line, terminator excluded: `awk '{sub(/\r$/,""); print length($0)}' F | sort | uniq -c`.
- CRLF lines: `grep -c $'\r$' F`; LF-only line numbers: `grep -vn $'\r$' F | cut -d: -f1`; final bytes: `tail -c 2 F | od -An -c`.
- File size: `wc -c F`. USRSEC line lengths: `awk 'NR>=35 && NR<=44 {print NR, length($0)}' app/jcl/DUSRSECJ.jcl`.
- Byte range, sign bytes, digit fields, dates, timestamps, FILLER contents and key order: a throwaway script that slices each line at the `copybooks.md` offsets (results in FIXFRM-015 to FIXFRM-021). The per-record results of the scenarios were derived by applying the cited rules record by record, with a scripted replay of the same rules as a cross-check only.
- The planner's scripted cross-check (262 posted, 38 rejected, every reason `0102`, first rejects at lines 16, 32, 35, 43, 50, 66, 79, 89, 101 and 129) agrees with the per-record derivation of FIXPOST-001 to FIXPOST-300; no difference was left to reconcile.

## Scenarios

- `LOADED`: a database freshly migrated by `carddemo-migrate`, then the nine loads in `scripts/dev-up.sh` order: `acctfile`, `cardfile`, `xreffile`, `custfile`, `discgrp`, `tcatbalf`, `trancatg`, `trantype`, `dusrsecj` (the order of `scripts/remote_refresh.sh:16-44` less TRANFILE, which is out of scope, AAP 0.2.3). `transactions`, `pending_auth_summaries`, `pending_auth_details`, `auth_fraud` and `cssl_error_log` are empty; schema `db2` holds only the seed of migration 0006 (E23); `var/` holds no generation file.
- `POSTED`: `LOADED`, then `carddemo-batch posttran` over `app/data/ASCII/dailytran.txt` with the `FixedClock` instant `2022-07-01 12:00:00.000000`. The instant lies inside the default TRANREPT window `2022-01-01` to `2022-07-06` and before the INTCALC PARM date `2022071800`. POSTTRAN writes it to every posted `TRAN-PROC-TS` as the layout `D` text `2022-07-01-12.00.00.000000` (two hundredths digits, then `0000`, PIC-019), stored as `2022-07-01 12:00:00` with form `D`. Tests compare time fields by format (AAP 0.5.6); the instant matters only because it decides the TRANREPT selection.
- `INTEREST-RAW`: `LOADED`, then `carddemo-batch intcalc --parm-date 2022071800` with the `FixedClock` instant `2022-07-18 00:00:00.000000`; every SYSTRAN timestamp is layout `D` and is compared by format.
- `INTEREST-POSTED`: `POSTED`, then the same `intcalc` run.
- `creastmt` and `tranrept` run over `POSTED`; `tranrept` with its default dates (`--start-date 2022-01-01 --end-date 2022-07-06`). Every generation directory under `var/` starts empty, so each output is generation `G0001`.

Consumers:

- `crates/copybook/tests/pic_roundtrip.rs` and `crates/domain/tests/codec_a_roundtrip.rs` (i8, no database): FIXFRM and FIXUSR. `crates/persistence/tests/codec_b_roundtrip.rs` and `crates/persistence/tests/schema.rs` (d9, live database): FIXFRM and FIXUSR.
- `crates/batch/tests/loads.rs`: FIXLOAD, over `LOADED` and a second run of the nine loads.
- `crates/batch/tests/posttran_i9.rs` (i9): FIXPOST over `POSTED`; `crates/domain/tests/financial_parity.rs` (i2): the tcatbal and account results of FIXPOST and the interest results of FIXINT.
- `crates/batch/tests/intcalc.rs`: FIXINT over `INTEREST-RAW` and `INTEREST-POSTED`.
- `crates/batch/tests/creastmt.rs`: FIXSTMT over `POSTED`. `crates/batch/tests/tranrept.rs`: FIXRPT over `POSTED`.
- `modern/scripts/e2e.sh` hard-codes the posted count of FIXPOST-901 and the `posttran` exit status of FIXPOST-903 as constants, each commented with the rule ID it comes from, and fails NAV's data preparation on any other value; `posttran_i9.rs` asserts both, with the reject set of FIXPOST-902 (AAP 0.9.4).

## Reading the table

- Rule ID: `FIXFRM-` file framing and corpus scans; `FIXUSR-` the USRSEC records; `FIXLOAD-` the loads; `FIXPOST-<nnn>` with nnn the `dailytran.txt` line, plus summaries 901 to 903; `FIXINT-<nnn>` with nnn the `tcatbal.txt` line, plus summaries 901 to 903; `FIXSTMT-<nnn>` with nnn the `cardxref.txt` line, which is also the statement's position, plus file-level rows 901 to 904; `FIXRPT-<nnn>` with nnn the `cardxref.txt` line of the card block, plus file-level rows 901 to 905.
- Amounts are decimal strings with two decimal places, a negative value with a leading ASCII `-`. `bal`, `cr`, `db` are `ACCT-CURR-BAL`, `ACCT-CURR-CYC-CREDIT`, `ACCT-CURR-CYC-DEBIT`; `limit` is `ACCT-CREDIT-LIMIT`; `exp` is `ACCT-EXPIRAION-DATE`, compared with `DALYTRAN-ORIG-TS(1:10)`, which is `2022-06-10` on every record (FIXFRM-017); `amt` is `DALYTRAN-AMT`; `temp` is `WS-TEMP-BAL` as stored. A `tran_cat_balances` key is written (account, type, category), shortened to `tcatbal`.
- `{T:n}` stands for the 16 bytes of `DALYTRAN-ID` on `dailytran.txt:n`, which become `TRAN-ID` when the record is posted; `{C:n}` stands for the 16-byte card number on `cardxref.txt:n`. Both are written by reference, card numbers under Cardholder Data Redaction and transaction IDs so that no run of 16 digits appears in this file. A quoted output line containing one of them stands for that line with the 16 bytes in place. SYSTRAN transaction IDs are written as their two parts, the PARM date and the six-digit suffix.
- Quoted output lines are exact, with trailing spaces omitted: every record is right-padded with spaces to its length (80 for `STATEMNT.PS`, 100 for `STATEMNT.HTML`, 133 for the report, 350 for SYSTRAN, 430 for DALYREJS) and followed by LF (AAP 0.3.4.3).
- Proof: every row is asserted by a test of its scenario carrying `// audit: <Rule ID>`. A value that a row names as covered by a halt or a halt candidate is reported blocked and asserted as parity only once the halt is resolved (Equivalence failure constraint).

## Rules governing this file

- Cardholder Data Redaction: no card number, SSN, CVV or password value appears here. Records are cited by `file:line`, card numbers as `{C:n}`, and password bytes by their position on the in-stream line. The statement, report, reject and SYSTRAN files carry full card numbers where the COBOL writes them; they are local files, not egress, and their expected bytes are given by reference.
- Behaviour constraint: audited defects appear as expected results: E08 (the last account is never rewritten), ARITH-033 (a negative cycle debit adds its magnitude to `temp`), the first-word rules of the CREASTMT `STRING` statements and the TRANREPT end-of-file double count. E09 (`0103` overwriting `0102`) and E10 (`WS-TEMP-BAL` truncation) hold as rules but no fixture record reaches them (FIXPOST-902).
- Typed Dates and Timestamps: every fixture date decodes to a valid date (FIXFRM-016); every `DALYTRAN-ORIG-TS` is layout `I` and every `DALYTRAN-PROC-TS` is blank, decoding to `None` (FIXFRM-017); the timestamps `posttran` and `intcalc` write are layout `D`.
- Local-Only Runtime: every scenario runs against a local database (testcontainers or the Compose stack) with the fixtures read in place; no external data is used.
- R7 — Anomaly handling: anomalies are cited by escalation ID or proposed below as `X-FIXTURES-<n>`, never normalised; a typed codec error met on a fixture is escalated, never worked around. R16: `app/**` and `scripts/**` are read only.

No reason `0100` (every card is on a `cardxref.txt` line, CBTRN02C-004), `0101` (every cross-referenced account is on an `acctdata.txt` line, CBTRN02C-005) or `0103` (every exp is at least `2023-01-06`, after `2022-06-10`, CBTRN02C-008) occurs, so E09 is not reached; every `temp` lies between -945.66 and 4440.42, so E10's truncation is not reached | CBTRN02C.cbl:176-182,393-422,446-465; POSTTRAN.jcl:34-38 | `dailytran.txt:16-299` (the 38 listed lines) |

| FIXINT-901 | CBACT04C | 1300-B-WRITE-TX | SYSTRAN record, `CVTRA05Y` layout (CVTRA05Y-001 to CVTRA05Y-014) | `INTEREST-RAW` and `INTEREST-POSTED`: every category-balance record whose rate is not 0.00, that is the 50 (account n, `01`, `0001`) records | `var/systran/SYSTRAN.G0001` holds 50 records of 350 bytes, each followed by LF, in key order, record n for account n; its path is printed. Record n: `TRAN-ID` the PARM date `2022071800` followed by the suffix n as six digits (`000001` to `000050`; the suffix counts written records across the run); `TRAN-TYPE-CD` `01`; `TRAN-CAT-CD` `0005`; `TRAN-SOURCE` `System` and 4 spaces; `TRAN-DESC` bytes 1-24 `Int. for a/c ` followed by the account ID, bytes 25-100 X-FIXTURES-H4; `TRAN-AMT` the monthly interest of FIXINT-n in zoned form (PIC-005, ARITH-012), 0.00 in `INTEREST-RAW`; `TRAN-MERCHANT-ID` `000000000`; merchant name, city and ZIP spaces; `TRAN-CARD-NUM` `{C:k}` for the `cardxref.txt` line k of the account; `TRAN-ORIG-TS` and `TRAN-PROC-TS` both the `FixedClock` text `2022-07-18-00.00.00.000000`, form `D` (PIC-019); FILLER X-FIXTURES-H4. The records are written to the file only, never to `transactions` (COMBTRAN is out of scope, AAP 0.2.3) | CBACT04C.cbl:473-515,613-626; INTCALC.jcl:22,37-41 | `tcatbal.txt:1-50`; `cardxref.txt:1-50` |

| FIXSTMT-901 | CBSTM03A | 8100-TRNXFILE-OPEN; 8500-READTRNX-READ; 1000-MAINLINE | `TRNX-RECORD` (`COSTM01.CPY`); `WS-TRNX-TABLE` (`OCCURS 51` cards × `OCCURS 10` transactions) | `POSTED`: the 262 `transactions` rows ordered by card number, then `tran_id` (STEP010 sort); `card_xref` read in card order; `accounts` and `customers` as `POSTED` leaves them | the transaction set is non-empty, so the first read succeeds and no `ERROR READING TRNXFILE` abend occurs; the table receives 50 cards with 1 to 6 transactions each, within its 51 × 10 bounds; 50 statements are written, one per `card_xref` row in card order, which is `cardxref.txt` line order, and every card has at least one transaction. `STATEMNT.PS` holds 1,212 records of 80 bytes (19 + n per statement with n transactions) and `STATEMNT.HTML` 6,082 records of 100 bytes (64 + 11n per statement; the program's 100, not STEP030's 80, E20), each record followed by LF; both have fixed names in `CARDDEMO_STATEMENT_DIR`, replacing any previous run's files, and their paths are printed; exit status 0. Statements 7, 30 and 47 have negative balances. No name part is blank or holds more than one word, so every `ST-NAME` is the three parts joined by one space; on 24 statements `CUST-ADDR-LINE-3` holds more than one word and `ST-LINE4` keeps only the first (rows FIXSTMT-001 to FIXSTMT-050) | CBSTM03A.CBL:293-339,416-456,730-853; CBSTM03B.CBL:116-230; CREASTMT.JCL:53-54,87-96 | `cardxref.txt:1-50`; `custdata.txt:1-50`; `acctdata.txt:1-50`; `dailytran.txt:1-300` |
| FIXSTMT-902 | CBSTM03A | 5000-CREATE-STATEMENT; 4000-TRNXFILE-GET; 6000-WRITE-TRANS | `STATEMENT-LINES` (`ST-LINE0` to `ST-LINE15`, `ST-LINE14A`), each 80 bytes | every statement of `POSTED` | records in this order: `ST-LINE0`, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 5, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 12, one `ST-LINE14` per transaction, then 12, 14A, 15. Fixed lines: `ST-LINE0` = 31 `*`, `START OF STATEMENT`, 31 `*`; `ST-LINE5`, `ST-LINE10`, `ST-LINE12` = 80 `-`; `ST-LINE6` = 33 spaces, `Basic Details`; `ST-LINE11` = 30 spaces, `TRANSACTION SUMMARY`; `ST-LINE13` = `Tran ID         Tran Details`, 41 spaces, `Tran Amount`; `ST-LINE15` = 32 `*`, `END OF STATEMENT`, 32 `*`. Variable lines, given per statement in FIXSTMT-001 to FIXSTMT-050: `ST-LINE1` `ST-NAME`; `ST-LINE2`, `ST-LINE3` the first two address lines as stored; `ST-LINE4` `ST-ADD3`; `ST-LINE7` `Account ID         :` and the account ID; `ST-LINE8` `Current Balance    :` and the balance edited `9(9).99-` (nine digits with leading zeros, `.`, two digits, `-` or a space); `ST-LINE9` `FICO Score         :` and the score; `ST-LINE14` the 16-byte `TRAN-ID`, a space, the first 49 bytes of `TRAN-DESC`, `$` and the amount edited `Z(9).99-` (leading zeros as spaces, `.`, two digits, `-` or a space); `ST-LINE14A` `Total EXP:`, 56 spaces, `$` and the card's total, the sum of its amounts stored `S9(9)V99`, edited `Z(9).99-` | CBSTM03A.CBL:85-146,416-504,675-681 | `cardxref.txt:1-50` |
| FIXSTMT-903 | CBSTM03A | 5100-WRITE-HTML-HEADER; 5200-WRITE-HTML-NMADBS; 6000-WRITE-TRANS; 4000-TRNXFILE-GET | `HTML-FIXED-LN X(100)` and its 88-level values; `HTML-L11`; `HTML-ADDR-LN`, `HTML-BSIC-LN`, `HTML-TRAN-LN X(100)` | every statement of `POSTED` | each statement is one complete document of 64 + 11n records. 5100 (22): L01 `<!DOCTYPE html>`, L02 `<html lang="en">`, L03 `<head>`, L04 `<meta charset="utf-8">`, L05 `<title>HTML Table Layout</title>`, L06 `</head>`, L07 `<body style="margin:0px;">`, L08 `<table  align="center" frame="box" style="width:70%; font:12px Segoe UI,sans-serif;">`, LTRS `<tr>`, L10 `<td colspan="3" style="padding:0px 5px;background-color:#1d1d96b3;">`, `HTML-L11` (FIXSTMT-001 to FIXSTMT-050), LTDE `</td>`, LTRE `</tr>`, LTRS, L15 `<td colspan="3" style="padding:0px 5px;background-color:#FFAF33;">`, L16 `<p style="font-size:16px">Bank of XYZ</p>`, L17 `<p>410 Terry Ave N</p>`, L18 `<p>Seattle WA 99999</p>`, LTDE, LTRE, LTRS, L22-35 `<td colspan="3" style="padding:0px 5px;background-color:#f2f2f2;">`. 5200 (34): the name record, the three address records, LTDE, LTRE, LTRS, L30-42 `<td colspan="3" style="padding:0px 5px;background-color:#33FFD1; text-align:center;">`, L31 `<p style="font-size:16px">Basic Details</p>`, LTDE, LTRE, LTRS, L22-35, `<p>Account ID         : ` + the 20-byte `ST-ACCT-ID` + `</p>`, `<p>Current Balance    : ` + the 13-byte `ST-CURR-BAL` + `</p>`, `<p>FICO Score         : ` + the 20-byte `ST-FICO-SCORE` + `</p>`, LTDE, LTRE, LTRS, L30-42, L43 `<p style="font-size:16px">Transaction Summary</p>`, LTDE, LTRE, LTRS, L47 `<td style="width:25%; padding:0px 5px; background-color:#33FF5E; text-align:left;">`, L48 `<p style="font-size:16px">Tran ID</p>`, LTDE, L50 (L47 with `width:55%`), L51 `<p style="font-size:16px">Tran Details</p>`, LTDE, L53 (L47 with `width:20%` and `text-align:right;`), L54 `<p style="font-size:16px">Amount</p>`, LTDE, LTRE. Per transaction (11): LTRS, L58 `<td style="width:25%; padding:0px 5px; background-color:#f2f2f2; text-align:left;">`, `<p>` + `ST-TRANID` + `</p>`, LTDE, L61 (L58 with `width:55%`), `<p>` + the 49-byte `ST-TRANDT` + `</p>`, LTDE, L64 (L58 with `width:20%` and `text-align:right;`), `<p>` + the 13-byte `ST-TRANAMT` + `</p>`, LTDE, LTRE. Trailer (8): LTRS, L10, L75 `<h3>End of Statement</h3>`, LTDE, LTRE, L78 `</table>`, L79 `</body>`, L80 `</html>`. ## Escalation candidates

Proposed under R7 — Anomaly handling. The reviewer folds each candidate into `ESCALATIONS.md` under the next free ID, as its `## Later entries` section describes; until then the rows above cite the candidate name.

- `X-FIXTURES-1`
  - Found in: `app/cbl/CBTRN02C.cbl:474-479,503-511`; `app/cpy/CVTRA01Y.cpy:10`.
  - Anomaly class: declaration-not-followed.
  - Finding: `2700-A-CREATE-TCATBAL-REC` builds a new category-balance record with `INITIALIZE TRAN-CAT-BAL-RECORD`, which, with no `FILLER` phrase, sets only the named fields and leaves the 22-byte `FILLER` unchanged. The working record's `FILLER` therefore holds the bytes of the last record a `READ TCATBAL-FILE INTO TRAN-CAT-BAL-RECORD` returned, because a read that ends `INVALID KEY` moves nothing. In `POSTED` the first category-balance access (FIXPOST-001) finds its row, and every loaded row holds 22 `0` characters, so all 50 created rows hold 22 `0` characters in `filler`, not spaces.
  - Proposed resolution: reproduced (Behaviour constraint): a created row takes the `FILLER` bytes of the row most recently read in the same run, which FIXPOST-901 states for the corpus. A run whose first access creates a row is X-FIXTURES-H2. The reviewer reconciles the `2700-A-CREATE-TCATBAL-REC` row of `batch/POSTTRAN.md` with this finding against `CBTRN02C.cbl:504` and IBM's `INITIALIZE` rules.

## Halt candidates

Cases found while deriving the rows above that have no single result. Each is proposed as a new halt or to join an existing one, and the reviewer folds it into `HALTS.md` under its `## Later entries` procedure. Until then, no parity assertion is written for the values a candidate names.

- `X-FIXTURES-H1`, proposed to join H2.
  - Path: `app/cbl/CBTRN02C.cbl:424-444,562-566`; `app/cpy/CVTRA05Y.cpy:18`. `TRAN-RECORD` is copied into WORKING-STORAGE with no `VALUE`; `2000-POST-TRANSACTION` moves every named field but never the 20-byte `FILLER`, nothing initialises the record, and the transaction file is opened for output only, so no read fills it.
  - Why no single result: the `FILLER` bytes of every posted transaction are the record's initial storage, which depends on runtime storage initialisation that nothing in the repository sets, as in H2.
  - Bindings blocked: g3b: the `transactions.filler` value of the 262 rows `posttran` writes in `POSTED` (FIXPOST-901). Every other column stays assertable; `creastmt` and `tranrept` never read the field.
  - Candidate resolution: as H2.
- `X-FIXTURES-H2`, proposed to join H2.
  - Path: `app/cbl/CBTRN02C.cbl:474-479,503-511`. When the first category-balance access of a run finds no row, `2700-A-CREATE-TCATBAL-REC` builds the new row from a working record no read has filled, so its `FILLER` is the initial storage (X-FIXTURES-1).
  - Why no single result: as H2.
  - Bindings blocked: g3b: the `filler` of the rows created in such a run. `POSTED` does not reach the case, because FIXPOST-001's key exists, so no row of this file is blocked.
  - Candidate resolution: as H2.
- `X-FIXTURES-H3`, proposed as the same case as the within-card order candidate of `batch/TRANREPT.md`.
  - Path: `app/jcl/TRANREPT.jcl:46-48` and `app/proc/TRANREPT.prc:44-46`: `SORT FIELDS=(TRAN-CARD-NUM,A)` with no `EQUALS`; `app/cbl/CBTRN03C.cbl:197-204,282-290`.
  - Why no single result: without `EQUALS`, DFSORT does not keep the input order of records with the same card number, and no repository source sets the installation default, so the order of the details inside each card block is not fixed. The page totals whose page boundary splits a card block, the pages after them, and the amount repeated at end of file follow that order.
  - Bindings blocked: g3b: the order of the details inside each block of FIXRPT-001 to FIXRPT-050, the page totals at records 81, 101, 121, 161, 181, 261, 281, 321, 381, 401, 441 and 449 (FIXRPT-904) and the grand total at record 451 (FIXRPT-905). Record kinds and positions, the detail texts of each block taken as a set, the 49 account totals and the page totals at records 21, 201 and 341 stay assertable. The values given hold for `EQUALS`, because the input is the transaction file unloaded in `tran_id` order.
  - Candidate resolution: IBM DFSORT documentation of the installation's `EQUALS` default, or a user decision.
- `X-FIXTURES-H4`, proposed to join H2, with the SYSTRAN halt candidates of `batch/INTCALC.md` if that file proposes the same case.
  - Path: `app/cbl/CBACT04C.cbl:473-500`; `app/cpy/CVTRA05Y.cpy:9,18`. `1300-B-WRITE-TX` strings 24 bytes into `TRAN-DESC PIC X(100)`, never moves the 20-byte `FILLER`, and nothing initialises `TRAN-RECORD`, which has no `VALUE`.
  - Why no single result: `TRAN-DESC` bytes 25-100 and the `FILLER` of every SYSTRAN record are the record's initial storage, as in H2.
  - Bindings blocked: g3b: those 96 bytes of each of the 50 SYSTRAN records of FIXINT-901, in both interest scenarios. Every other byte of the records stays assertable.
  - Candidate resolution: as H2.
